  Ambiguity in CLAUDE.md to address before Phase 2

  1. Section numbering mismatch: CLAUDE.md says "NCO v11.0 §4" for directory
  structure, but the actual NCO document uses Roman numerals — it's Section 
  VI.B, Table 3 ("Code Delivery and Vertical Structure → Directory Structures").
   I used the correct section references in all rule citations.
  2. 8 dirs vs. 14 dirs: The NCO Table 3 lists 14 subdirectories (doc, jobs,
  scripts, ush, sorc, exec, parm, parm/wmo, fix, lib, ecf, gempak, versions,
  modulefiles). CLAUDE.md specifies only 8. I went with the 8 specified in
  CLAUDE.md since some (doc, exec, fix, lib, gempak) are arguably optional
  depending on whether the model has that type of content. You may want to add
  some as WARN-level checks in a later phase.
  3. Self-check: Running r2o-check lint . on the r2o-check repo itself will fail
   all 8 structure checks (since r2o-check is a Python tool, not an operational
  model package). The "Definition of Done" says self-check should pass. You
  likely want to either add disabled_rules to the root .r2o-check.yml or decide
  that self-check only applies from Phase 5+ when the tool has awareness of its
  own repo type.

✻ Crunched for 7m 20s

❯ Excellent first phase — 97% coverage, clean commits, and the kinds of pointed 
   questions back at you that show Claude Code was actually engaging with the   
  spec rather than just executing it. Let me address each ambiguity so you can  
  update CLAUDE.md before Phase 2.                                              
                                                                                
  ## 1. Section numbering — fix in CLAUDE.md                                    
                                                                                
  Claude Code is right. NCO Implementation Standards v11.0 uses Roman numerals, 
   and directory structure lives in **§VI.B, Table 3**, not §4. Update every    
  reference in CLAUDE.md to use the correct section. This matters because rule  
  documentation cites these sections and reviewers (eventually NCO SPAs) will   
  check them.                                                                   
                                                                                
  **Action:** Find/replace in CLAUDE.md: `NCO v11.0 §4` → `NCO v11.0 §VI.B      
  Table 3` for directory structure rules. Do the same audit for any other       
  section refs as you add rules in later phases — ask Claude Code to verify     
  section numbers against the live document at the start of each phase.         
                                                                                
  ## 2. 8 dirs vs. 14 dirs — this is the more interesting question              
                                                                                
  Claude Code surfaced a real design decision. The 14 directories in Table 3    
  split into two categories:                                                    
                                                                                
  **Always required** (FAIL if missing): `jobs/`, `scripts/`, `ush/`, `sorc/`,  
  `parm/`, `ecf/`, `versions/`, `modulefiles/` — these are the 8 you already    
  have.                                                                         
                                                                                
  **Conditionally required** (WARN if missing): `doc/`, `exec/`, `fix/`,        
  `lib/`, `gempak/`, `parm/wmo/` — depend on whether the package has compiled   
  binaries, fixed data, GEMPAK output, WMO headers, etc.                        
                                                                                
  This is the right model. **Add the 6 conditional directories as WARN-level    
  rules in Phase 2**, not later. They're cheap to add now while structure is    
  fresh, and they make r2o-check meaningfully more complete. Rule IDs           
  `R2OSTR009`–`R2OSTR014`. Document each with the specific condition under      
  which the directory becomes mandatory (e.g., "FAIL if `sorc/` contains        
  Fortran sources but `exec/` is missing").                                     
                                                                                
  **Action:** Add a new bullet to Phase 2 in CLAUDE.md: *"Extend structure      
  rules with 6 conditional directories (R2OSTR009–R2OSTR014) at WARN severity   
  by default, with conditional FAIL escalation documented per rule."*           
                                                                                
  ## 3. Self-check — Claude Code caught a real architectural issue              
                                                                                
  This is the most important of the three. The "self-check must pass"           
  requirement in CLAUDE.md was sloppy on my part — r2o-check is a Python tool,  
  not an operational model package, so of course it has no `jobs/` or `ecf/`    
  directory. Forcing it to pass would either require disabling every rule       
  (defeats the point) or adding fake directories (worse).                       
                                                                                
  The right fix is to introduce a **repo type** concept now, before it          
  metastasizes:                                                                 
                                                                                
  ```yaml                                                                       
  # .r2o-check.yml                                                              
  repo_type: tool   # one of: operational_model, workflow, tool, library        
  standards_version: "11.0"                                                     
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  Rules declare which repo types they apply to. Structure rules apply to        
  `operational_model` and `workflow` only. When you run `r2o-check lint .` on   
  the r2o-check repo with `repo_type: tool`, no structure rules execute and the 
   self-check passes meaningfully — it verifies the tool runs cleanly, not that 
   the tool pretends to be an operational model.                                
                                                                                
  This also future-proofs r2o-check for the reality that NOAA repos aren't all  
  the same shape. global-workflow is a `workflow`, STOFS-operational is an      
  `operational_model`, ufs-weather-model is a `model_source`, and a Python      
  utility like uwtools is a `tool`.                                             
                                                                                
  **Action:** Add to Phase 2 in CLAUDE.md:                                      
                                                                                
  > **Introduce `repo_type` to config.** Add `repo_type: operational_model |    
  workflow | tool | library | model_source` field to `.r2o-check.yml` (default: 
   `operational_model`). Each rule's `@register_rule` decorator takes an        
  `applies_to: list[RepoType]` parameter; the runner skips rules whose          
  `applies_to` doesn't include the current repo's type. Existing structure      
  rules (R2OSTR001–008) apply to `[operational_model, workflow]`. The r2o-check 
   repo's own `.r2o-check.yml` sets `repo_type: tool`, and self-check then      
  passes by virtue of having no applicable rules — which is correct behavior,   
  not a workaround.

      208 +def _has_fortran_sources(repo_path: Path) -> bool:            
      209 +    """Check if sorc/ contains Fortran source files."""       
      210 +    sorc = repo_path / "sorc"                                 
      211 +    if not sorc.is_dir():                                     
      212 +        return False                                          
      213 +    for ext in (".f", ".f90", ".F", ".F90", ".f77", ".ftn"):  
      214 +        if list(sorc.rglob(f"*{ext}")):                       
      215 +            return True                                       
      216 +    return False                                              
      217 +                                                              
      218 +                                                              
      219 +def _has_c_sources(repo_path: Path) -> bool:                  
      220 +    """Check if sorc/ contains C/C++ source files."""         
      221 +    sorc = repo_path / "sorc"                                 
      222 +    if not sorc.is_dir():                                     
      223 +        return False                                          
      224 +    for ext in (".c", ".cpp", ".cc", ".C", ".cxx"):           
      225 +        if list(sorc.rglob(f"*{ext}")):                       
      226 +            return True                                       
      227 +    return False

          -in the `modulefiles/` subdirectory. WCOSS uses the Lmod enviro
          -nmental module system (Section VI.A.5). |                     
      149 +| **Rationale** | WCOSS uses Lmod; module files must be Lua fo
          +rmat in `modulefiles/` (§VI.A.5). |                           
      150

      225 +                                                              
      226 +## Naming Rules (R2ONAM)                                      
      227 +                                                              
      228 +These rules validate file naming conventions for J-jobs, ex-sc
          +ripts,                                                        
      229 +module files, and version files.                              
      230 +                                                              
      231 +**Applies to:** `operational_model`, `workflow`               
      232 +                                                              
      233 +---                                                           
      234 +                                                              
      235 +### R2ONAM001 — J-job file naming                             
      236 +                                                              
      237 +| Field | Value |                                             
      238 +|---|---|                                                     
      239 +| **Severity** | FAIL |                                       
      240 +| **NCO Section** | IV.C |                                    
      241 +| **Rationale** | J-jobs must follow `JAAAAA` — all caps, begi
          +nning with J, no extension. Underscores permitted. |          
      242 +                                                              
      243 +**Example failure:**                                          
      244 +```                                                           
      245 +✘ FAIL  R2ONAM001  J-job 'jmodel_forecast' violates naming con
          +vention                                                       
      246 +```                                                           
      247 +                                                              
      248 +**Fix:** Rename to `JMODEL_FORECAST` (all uppercase, starts wi
          +th J, no extension).                                          
      249 +                                                              
      250 +**Validated patterns:** `JRRFS_FORECAST`, `JSTOFS_2D_GLO_POST`
      251 +                                                              
      252 +---                                                           
      253 +                                                              
      254 +### R2ONAM002 — Ex-script file naming                         
      255 +                                                              
      256 +| Field | Value |                                             
      257 +|---|---|                                                     
      258 +| **Severity** | FAIL |                                       
      259 +| **NCO Section** | IV.C |                                    
      260 +| **Rationale** | Ex-scripts must follow `exaaaaa.sh` — all lo
          +wercase, beginning with `ex`, ending with `.sh`/`.pl`/`.py`. |
      261 +                                                              
      262 +**Example failure:**                                          
      263 +```                                                           
      264 +✘ FAIL  R2ONAM002  Ex-script 'model_task.sh' violates naming  
      265 +```                                                           
      266 +                                                              
      267 +**Fix:** Rename to `exmodel_task.sh` (all lowercase, starts wi
          +th `ex`).                                                     
      268 +                                                              
      269 +**Validated patterns:** `exrrfs_forecast.sh`, `exstofs_2d_glo_
          +post.sh`                                                      
      270 +                                                              
      271 +---                                                           
      272 +                                                              
      273 +### R2ONAM003 — Modulefile format (.lua)                      
      274 +                                                              
      275 +| Field | Value |                                             
      276 +|---|---|                                                     
      277 +| **Severity** | FAIL |                                       
      278 +| **NCO Section** | VI.A.5 |                                  
      279 +| **Rationale** | WCOSS uses Lmod; all module files must be Lu
          +a format with `.lua` extension. |                             
      280 +                                                              
      281 +**Example failure:**                                          
      282 +```                                                           
      283 +✘ FAIL  R2ONAM003  Modulefile 'model.tcl' is not .lua format  
      284 +```                                                           
      285 +                                                              
      286 +**Fix:** Convert to Lua format with `.lua` extension.         
      287 +                                                              
      288 +---                                                           
      289 +                                                              
      290 +### R2ONAM004 — Version file naming and format                
      291 +                                                              
      292 +| Field | Value |                                             
      293 +|---|---|                                                     
      294 +| **Severity** | FAIL (missing file) / WARN (bad format) |    
      295 +| **NCO Section** | VI.B, Table 3 |                           
      296 +| **Rationale** | `versions/` must contain `run.ver` and `buil
          +d.ver`. Each line must be `export var=value` or a comment. |  
      297 +                                                              
      298 +**Example failure:**                                          
      299 +```                                                           
      300 +✘ FAIL  R2ONAM004  Missing version file 'build.ver'           
      301 +⚠ WARN  R2ONAM004  run.ver:1: line does not match 'export var=
          +value'                                                        
      302 +```                                                           
      303 +                                                              
      304 +**Fix:** Create missing files. Ensure all lines use `export va
          +r=value` format.

  Ambiguities / decisions for Phase 3

  1. Conditional FAIL escalation for R2OSTR009, 011–014: Currently only exec/
  (R2OSTR010) has escalation logic (Fortran/C in sorc/). The others are always
  WARN. Should any escalate? For example: gempak/ FAIL if the model produces
  GEMPAK output? parm/wmo/ FAIL if WMO-headed products are generated? This would
   require inspecting output patterns or config hints — likely too speculative
  for now.
  2. STOFS nested scripts directory: Real STOFS puts ex-scripts in
  scripts/stofs_2d_glo/ (one level deeper), not directly in scripts/. R2ONAM002
  currently checks direct children of scripts/ only. Should it recurse into
  subdirectories? This would be a Phase 3 decision.
  3. Ush-script naming rule: The NCO standard (§IV.C) specifies ush scripts must
   be all lowercase, NOT begin with ex, and end with .sh/.pl/.py. I didn't add a
   separate R2ONAM005 for this because the requirement is loose (any lowercase
  name that isn't an ex-script). Worth adding in Phase 3 or leaving as
  informational?
  4. model_source repo type: Currently gets 0 rules, same as tool and library.
  You may want some subset of rules (e.g., build-related rules from Phase 3) to
  apply to model_source. Worth defining now or waiting until Phase 3 has the
  build rules?

✻ Brewed for 32s

❯ Another strong phase — 98 tests, clean typing, self-check working correctly,  
  and four genuinely useful questions back. All four deserve answers before you 
   kick off Phase 3. Let me take them in order of impact.                       
  1. Conditional FAIL escalation for the other 5 dirs — defer, but document     
  Claude Code's instinct is right: escalating gempak/ or parm/wmo/ to FAIL      
  would require inspecting output configs or product generation logic, which is 
   speculative and would generate false positives on any model that produces    
  these outputs via a non-standard path. Leave them as WARN for now.            
  But don't leave the escalation logic undocumented. Add a short note in        
  docs/rules.md under each of R2OSTR009, 011–014 explaining why it's WARN-only  
  and what conditions would justify FAIL escalation in a future version. This   
  does two things: it shows reviewers you thought about it deliberately, and it 
   gives future contributors (or you in 6 months) a clear path to tighten the   
  rule when you have signal.                                                    
  Also add a one-liner to CLAUDE.md's "Do Not" section: "Do NOT add conditional 
   FAIL escalation logic that requires inspecting model output or runtime       
  behavior — r2o-check is a static checker."                                    
  2. Nested scripts/ directory — fix it, this is a real compliance issue        
  This is the most important of the four. If R2ONAM002 misses STOFS because it  
  doesn't recurse into scripts/stofs_2d_glo/, then r2o-check fails on the most  
  important repo you personally care about. That's a bug, not a design choice.  
  Go recursive. But recursive needs rules:                                      
                                                                                
  Recurse into scripts/ and any subdirectories                                  
  For each .sh/.py/.pl file, check if it matches the ex-script pattern          
  (ex<model>_<task>.sh)                                                         
  Only flag files that look like they're intended to be ex-scripts — i.e.,      
  files that start with ex or are directly referenced by a J-job. Don't         
  false-positive on helper scripts that happen to live in scripts/.             
                                                                                
  The cleanest heuristic: if a file in scripts/ (at any depth) starts with ex,  
  it must match ex<model>_<task>.(sh|py|pl). This gives you STOFS-style nested  
  compliance without false positives on scripts/utils/helper.sh.                
  Add a fixture for this in Phase 3. Create tests/fixtures/stofs_nested/ with   
  scripts/stofs_2d_glo/exstofs_2d_glo_nowcast.sh that should pass, and          
  scripts/stofs_2d_glo/exstofs_badname.sh that should fail. This protects you   
  from regression and documents the intended behavior.                          
  Action: Add to Phase 3 in CLAUDE.md:                                          
                                                                                
  Fix R2ONAM002 to recurse into scripts/ subdirectories. Files starting with ex 
   at any depth under scripts/ must match ex<model>_<task>.(sh|py|pl). Non-ex   
  files in scripts/ are not flagged by R2ONAM002. Add                           
  tests/fixtures/stofs_nested/ fixture exercising the STOFS one-level-deep      
  pattern and a negative case.                                                  
                                                                                
  3. Ush-script naming — add it, but as WARN                                    
  NCO §IV.C specifies that ush scripts must be all lowercase, not begin with    
  ex, and end with .sh/.pl/.py. The "loose" nature of the rule isn't a reason   
  to skip it — it's a reason to make it WARN instead of FAIL. NOAA standards    
  explicitly say this, so r2o-check should check it.                            
  Add R2ONAM005 in Phase 3 at WARN severity: "ush/ scripts should be lowercase, 
   must not start with 'ex', and must end with .sh/.pl/.py." The "should"       
  language in the rule message mirrors the NCO standard's tone. Easy rule,      
  small test surface, real value.                                               
  4. model_source repo type — decide now, save headaches later                  
  Claude Code is flagging a scope question that will recur in every future      
  phase: which repo types do the new build/environment/ecflow rules apply to?   
  Better to decide the taxonomy now.                                            
  My recommendation — codify this mapping in CLAUDE.md so you (and Claude Code) 
   never re-litigate it:                                                        
  Repo typeApplies tooperational_modelStructure, naming, environment, modules,  
  build, versions, ecflowworkflowStructure, naming, environment, modules,       
  versions, ecflowmodel_sourceBuild, modules (source code repos like            
  ufs-weather-model — no operational delivery structure but must compile        
  cleanly with standard targets)libraryBuild (libraries only need to build      
  cleanly)tool(no rules apply — self-check passes by having nothing to check)   
  The key insight: model_source gets Phase 3's build rules but not              
  structure/naming rules. This matches reality — ufs-weather-model has a

  NCO v11.0 §III.A Table 1 — Standard Environment Variables:

  ┌────────────────┬──────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    Variable    │  Where Set   │               Description               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ envir          │ job card     │ "test", "para", or "prod"               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ PACKAGEROOT    │ job card     │ Root dir for application                │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ OPSROOT        │ job card     │ Operations root directory               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ job            │ job card     │ Unique job name per cycle               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ jobid          │ job card     │ Unique job identifier ($job.$PBS_JOBID) │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ NET            │ J-job        │ Model name (1st level of com)           │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ RUN            │ J-job        │ Model run name (3rd level of com)       │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ PDY            │ J-job        │ Date YYYYMMDD                           │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ cyc            │ job card     │ Cycle time HH                           │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ cycle          │ J-job        │ Cycle time tHHz or tHHMMz               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ subcyc         │ job card     │ Cycle time minutes MM                   │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DATAROOT       │ job card     │ Working directory parent                │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DATA           │ J-job        │ Job working directory                   │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ HOMEmodel      │ job card     │ Application home directory              │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ USHmodel       │ J-job        │ Model's ush files location              │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ EXECmodel      │ J-job        │ Model's exec files location             │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ PARMmodel      │ J-job        │ Model's parm files location             │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ FIXmodel       │ J-job        │ Model's fix files location              │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ COMROOT        │ job card     │ com root directory                      │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ COMIN          │ J-job        │ Input com directory                     │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ COMOUT         │ J-job        │ Output com directory                    │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ COMINmodel     │ J-job        │ Incoming data from model                │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ COMOUTmodel    │ J-job        │ Outgoing data for model                 │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DCOMROOT       │ job card     │ dcom root directory                     │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DCOMIN         │ J-job        │ dcom input directory                    │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DCOMINdatatype │ J-job        │ dcom incoming by datatype               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ DBNROOT        │ job card     │ Data-alerting utilities root            │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SENDECF        │ job card     │ Boolean: ecflow_client commands         │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SENDDBN        │ job card     │ Boolean: send products off WCOSS        │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SENDDBN_NTC    │ job card     │ Boolean: send WMO products              │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SENDCOM        │ job card     │ Boolean: copy to $COMOUT                │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ SENDWEB        │ job card     │ Boolean: send to web server             │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ model_ver      │ job card     │ Package version (3 digits)              │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ module_ver     │ version file │ Runtime module version                  │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ extmodel_ver   │ version file │ External model dependency version       │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ KEEPDATA       │ job card     │ Boolean: keep working dir               │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ MAILTO         │ job card     │ Email addresses                         │
  ├────────────────┼──────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ MAILCC         │ job card     │ CC email addresses                      │
  └────────────────┴──────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Makefile targets (§VI.A.8): all, debug, install, clean (required); test
  (required for libraries/utilities).

  That's 38 variables. For R2OENV rules, I'll check J-jobs for the J-job-set
  variables (NET, RUN, PDY, cycle, DATA, USHmodel, EXECmodel, PARMmodel,
  FIXmodel, COMIN, COMOUT) since those are the ones the standard mandates J-jobs
   define. The job-card variables are set externally and not the package's
  responsibility to define.

● Update(src/r2o_check/rules/naming.py)
  ⎿  Added 57 lines
      138  
      139  
      140  @register_rule(applies_to=MODEL_REPO_TYPES)
      141 +def check_ush_naming(                                         
      142 +    repo_path: Path, config: Config                           
      143 +) -> list[LintResult]:                                        
      144 +    """R2ONAM005 — Validate ush/ script naming conventions.   
      145 +                                                              
      146 +    NCO v11.0 Section IV.C: ush scripts must be all lowercase,
      147 +    must not begin with 'ex', and must end with .sh, .pl, or .
          +py.                                                           
      148 +    """                                                       
      149 +    ush_dir = repo_path / "ush"                               
      150 +    if not ush_dir.is_dir():                                  
      151 +        return []                                             
      152 +                                                              
      153 +    results: list[LintResult] = []                            
      154 +    for f in sorted(ush_dir.rglob("*")):                      
      155 +        if not f.is_file():                                   
      156 +            continue                                          
      157 +        name = f.name                                         
      158 +        has_valid_ext = _USH_PATTERN.match(name) is not None  
      159 +        starts_with_ex = name.startswith("ex")                
      160 +        is_lowercase = name == name.lower()                   
      161 +                                                              
      162 +        if has_valid_ext and not starts_with_ex and is_lowerca
          +se:                                                           
      163 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      164 +                status=Status.PASS,                           
      165 +                rule_id="R2ONAM005",                          
      166 +                message=(                                     
      167 +                    f"Ush script '{name}' follows naming."    
      168 +                    " [NCO v11.0 IV.C]"                       
      169 +                ),                                            
      170 +                path=f,                                       
      171 +            ))                                                
      172 +        else:                                                 
      173 +            parts: list[str] = []                             
      174 +            if not is_lowercase:                              
      175 +                parts.append("must be lowercase")             
      176 +            if starts_with_ex:                                
      177 +                parts.append("must not start with 'ex'")      
      178 +            if not has_valid_ext:                             
      179 +                parts.append("must end with .sh/.pl/.py")     
      180 +            reason = "; ".join(parts)                         
      181 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      182 +                status=Status.WARN,                           
      183 +                rule_id="R2ONAM005",                          
      184 +                message=(                                     
      185 +                    f"Ush script '{name}': {reason}."         
      186 +                    " [NCO v11.0 IV.C]"                       
      187 +                ),                                            
      188 +                path=f,                                       
      189 +                fix_hint=(                                    
      190 +                    "Rename to lowercase, no 'ex' prefix,"    
      191 +                    " with .sh/.pl/.py extension."            
      192 +                ),                                            
      193 +            ))                                                
      194 +    return results                                            
      195 +                                                              
      196 +

  Numbers

  - 128 tests, 97% coverage

  - Total: 25 rules (R2OSTR001–014, R2ONAM001–005, R2OENV001, R2OBLD001,
  R2OMOD001, R2OVER001–002)

  Remaining ambiguities

  1. R2OENV001 severity: Currently WARN because many J-jobs legitimately omit
  some vars (e.g., a post-processing job may not set FIX*). The NCO standard
  says "variables that are not used in a given job need not be defined." Should
  there be a distinction between "core" vars (NET, RUN, PDY — always required)
  at FAIL and "optional" vars (FIX*, PARM* — context-dependent) at WARN?
  2. R2OBLD001 and CMake: The rule currently only finds
  Makefile/makefile/GNUmakefile. RRFS-workflow and global-workflow use CMake
  (CMakeLists.txt). Should R2OBLD001 also check for cmake --build target
  equivalents, or is that a separate rule?
  3. R2ONAM004 vs R2OVER001/002 overlap: R2ONAM004 checks version file presence
  + basic format; R2OVER001/002 does deeper content validation. These could be
  merged. Currently they coexist — R2ONAM004 is the "does it exist?" check,
  R2OVER does "is the content valid?" Keep them separate, or merge into R2OVER?

✻ Crunched for 12m 0s

❯ Phase 3 lands clean — 128 tests, 25 rules, all four rule categories           
  implemented, and the ambiguities coming back are now design questions rather  
  than spec gaps. That's a good sign: the architecture is holding up and        
  CLAUDE.md is close to stable. All three questions deserve quick, decisive     
  answers so you can hit Phase 4 (ecFlow) without architectural drag.           
  1. R2OENV001 severity split — yes, split it                                   
  Claude Code is right, and it's pointing at a real problem with                
  single-severity environment checks: a monolithic WARN rule is too noisy to    
  act on, but a monolithic FAIL rule would produce false positives on           
  post-processing jobs that legitimately don't need FIX* or PARM*. The NCO      
  standard itself acknowledges this by saying "variables that are not used in a 
   given job need not be defined."                                              
  Split into two rules:                                                         
                                                                                
  R2OENV001 (FAIL): Core variables that every J-job must set — NET, RUN, cycle, 
   PDY, DATA, COMIN, COMOUT. These are non-negotiable per §III.A.               
  R2OENV002 (WARN): Context-dependent variables — USH*, EXEC*, PARM*, FIX*.     
  Flagged when absent, but at WARN because absence is valid for some job types. 
                                                                                
  The split also sets you up for a future refinement: in a later version,       
  R2OENV002 could become FAIL when the J-job body actually references $USHmodel 
   or $PARMmodel without having set it — that's the kind of cross-reference     
  check that's worth doing eventually but doesn't need to block Phase 4.        
  Action: Update CLAUDE.md's rule inventory to reflect the R2OENV001/R2OENV002  
  split, and add this pattern as a note: "When a rule's severity genuinely      
  depends on context, split it into core-FAIL and context-WARN variants rather  
  than picking one compromise severity."                                        
  2. CMake support in R2OBLD001 — yes, but make it a separate rule              
  Don't overload R2OBLD001. Make Makefile and CMake separate rules because the  
  standard operational-delivery pattern is Makefile-based (NCO §VI.A.8          
  implicitly assumes make all, make install), while CMake is the modern         
  convention in model-source repos like global-workflow, ufs-weather-model, and 
   RRFS-workflow.                                                               
                                                                                
  R2OBLD001 (existing, FAIL): Makefile targets all, debug, install, clean.      
  Applies to operational_model, workflow — matches NCO delivery expectations.   
  R2OBLD002 (new, WARN): CMakeLists.txt must define equivalent targets or       
  install/build rules reachable via cmake --build . --target                    
  {all,install,clean}. Applies to operational_model, workflow, model_source,    
  library.                                                                      
                                                                                
  A repo only needs to satisfy one of the two — add logic in the runner (or in  
  the rule itself) so that if a valid Makefile is present and passes R2OBLD001, 
   R2OBLD002 is skipped, and vice versa. This prevents false positives on repos 
   that use one or the other exclusively.                                       
  Action: Add to Phase 4 in CLAUDE.md:                                          
                                                                                
  Add R2OBLD002 (CMake build target validation, WARN). Mutually exclusive with  
  R2OBLD001 — if a valid Makefile exists, R2OBLD002 is skipped; if a valid      
  CMakeLists.txt exists, R2OBLD001 is skipped. Rule signals implement this via  
  a new mutually_exclusive_with: list[str] parameter on @register_rule.         
                                                                                
  That new decorator parameter is general-purpose and will likely be useful     
  again (e.g., ecFlow .def vs .yaml suite definitions in Phase 4).              
  3. R2ONAM004 vs R2OVER001/002 overlap — merge, keep as R2OVER                 
  Claude Code's framing ("presence check vs content check") is the giveaway     
  that this is a layering issue, not a meaningful separation. In a mature       
  linter, presence and content checks for the same file live in the same module 
   and share severity logic. Having R2ONAM004 in the naming module and          
  R2OVER001/002 in a versions module creates:                                   
                                                                                
  Two places a user has to look up version-file compliance                      
  Duplicate citation to the same NCO section                                    
  Confusion about why the "naming" rule checks content format                   
                                                                                
  Merge R2ONAM004 into R2OVER. The consolidated structure:                      
                                                                                
  R2OVER001 (FAIL): versions/run.ver exists (was R2ONAM004 presence check, now  
  FAIL severity per Table 3 requirements)                                       
  R2OVER002 (FAIL): versions/build.ver exists                                   
  R2OVER003 (WARN): run.ver content follows export *_ver format (was old        
  R2OVER001)                                                                    
  R2OVER004 (WARN): build.ver content follows export *_ver format (was old      
  R2OVER002)                                                                    
                                                                                
  This means retiring R2ONAM004. That's a breaking change for anyone who        
  already pinned to r2o-check (nobody has, you're pre-release), so do it now    
  while it's free. Add a one-line entry in CHANGELOG.md under an "Unreleased /  
  Breaking" heading noting R2ONAM004 was merged into R2OVER001-002.             
  Action: Add to Phase 4 in CLAUDE.md:                                          
                                                                                
  Retire R2ONAM004 and merge its logic into R2OVER001 (presence) and R2OVER002  
  (presence). Renumber R2OVER001/002 content checks to R2OVER003/004. All four  
  apply to [operational_model, workflow]. Document the change in CHANGELOG.md   
  under "Breaking changes (pre-release)."                                       
                                                                                
  Phase 4 kickoff prompt                                                        
  Phase 4 is the heaviest phase technically — ecFlow parsing is substantive and 
   you'll want clean test coverage of edge cases. But before the ecFlow work,   
  do the three fixups above as housekeeping so Phase 4 starts from a consistent 
   base.                                                                        
  After updating CLAUDE.md, /clear and paste:                                   
                                                                                
  Read CLAUDE.md fully. Phase 4 starts with three housekeeping tasks from Phase 
   3 feedback, then the main ecFlow validation work.

  Update tests and docs.

  R2OBLD002 is skipped, and vice versa. Update LintRunner to honor the

  Retire R2ONAM004. Merge presence checks into R2OVER001 (run.ver) and

  Phase 4 main work — ecFlow validation:                                        
                                                                                
  Implement ecFlow .ecf file validation as text parsing (do NOT require the     
  ecFlow Python library). Rules R2OECF001 onward:                               
                                                                                
  R2OECF001 (FAIL): .ecf files must contain %include <head.h> and %include      
  <tail.h> directives                                                           
  R2OECF002 (FAIL): .ecf files must contain paired ecflow_client --init and     
  ecflow_client --complete calls                                                
  R2OECF003 (FAIL): .ecf files must set a trap handler (trap ... EXIT or        
  equivalent)                                                                   
  R2OECF004 (WARN): PBS/Slurm directives in .ecf files must be well-formed      
  (valid #PBS -l / #SBATCH syntax)                                              
  R2OECF005 (WARN): .ecf files should not contain hard-coded absolute paths     
  (flag /lfs/, /work/, /scratch/, /gpfs/ outside of variable assignments)       
                                                                                
                                                                                
  Add an optional deep-parse mode: if the ecflow Python package is importable,  
  parse .def suite definition files and validate trigger expressions and        
  dependency graph acyclicity. Rules R2OECF006–R2OECF00N gated behind the       
  optional import. Gracefully skip (not fail) if ecflow library is unavailable, 
   reporting "deep ecFlow checks skipped — install pip install ecflow to        
  enable."                                                                      
  Apply ecFlow rules to [operational_model, workflow] per the repo type matrix. 
  Add fixtures: tests/fixtures/ecflow_compliant/ with well-formed .ecf files,   
  tests/fixtures/ecflow_broken/ missing head/tail includes and with unpaired    
  ecflow_client calls, tests/fixtures/ecflow_hardcoded_paths/ for R2OECF005.    
  Update docs/rules.md with all new rules, exact NCO section citations (verify  
  against the live document — do not guess), rationale, example failures,       
  example fixes.                                                                
  Update CHANGELOG.md. Maintain >85% coverage. Commit the ecFlow work in        
  logical chunks separate from the housekeeping commit.

      124 +def _find_cmake_files(repo_path: Path) -> list[Path]:         
      125 +    """Find CMakeLists.txt under sorc/."""                    
      126 +    sorc = repo_path / "sorc"                                 
      127 +    if not sorc.is_dir():                                     
      128 +        return []                                             
      129 +    return sorted(sorc.rglob("CMakeLists.txt"))               
      130 +                                                              
      131 +                                                              
      132 +@register_rule(                                               
      133 +    applies_to=BUILD_REPO_TYPES,                              
      134 +    mutually_exclusive_with=["R2OBLD001"],                    
      135 +)                                                             
      136 +def check_cmake_build(                                        
      137 +    repo_path: Path, config: Config                           
      138 +) -> list[LintResult]:                                        
      139 +    """R2OBLD002 — Check CMake build system.                  
      140 +                                                              
      141 +    NCO v11.0 Section VI.A.8 equivalent for CMake: project()  
      142 +    and install() directives should be present. WARN severity 
      143 +    because CMake is the modern convention but NCO §VI.A.8    
      144 +    explicitly references Makefile targets.                   
      145 +    """                                                       
      146 +    cmake_files = _find_cmake_files(repo_path)                
      147 +    if not cmake_files:                                       
      148 +        sorc = repo_path / "sorc"                             
      149 +        if not sorc.is_dir():                                 
      150 +            return []                                         
      151 +        return [LintResult(                                   
      152 +            status=Status.WARN,                               
      153 +            rule_id="R2OBLD002",                              
      154 +            message=(                                         
      155 +                "No CMakeLists.txt found under sorc/."        
      156 +                " [NCO v11.0 VI.A.8]"                         
      157 +            ),                                                
      158 +            path=sorc,                                        
      159 +            fix_hint="Add a CMakeLists.txt or Makefile.",     
      160 +        )]                                                    
      161 +                                                              
      162 +    results: list[LintResult] = []                            
      163 +    for cf in cmake_files:                                    
      164 +        content = cf.read_text(                               
      165 +            encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"                
      166 +        )                                                     
      167 +        rel = cf.relative_to(repo_path)                       
      168 +                                                              
      169 +        for label, pattern in CMAKE_EXPECTED_PATTERNS:        
      170 +            if re.search(pattern, content):                   
      171 +                results.append(LintResult(                    
      172 +                    status=Status.PASS,                       
      173 +                    rule_id="R2OBLD002",                      
      174 +                    message=(                                 
      175 +                        f"{rel}: has '{label}' directive."    
      176 +                        " [NCO v11.0 VI.A.8]"                 
      177 +                    ),                                        
      178 +                    path=cf,                                  
      179 +                ))                                            
      180 +            else:                                             
      181 +                results.append(LintResult(                    
      182 +                    status=Status.WARN,                       
      183 +                    rule_id="R2OBLD002",                      
      184 +                    message=(                                 
      185 +                        f"{rel}: missing '{label}' directive."
      186 +                        " [NCO v11.0 VI.A.8]"                 
      187 +                    ),                                        
      188 +                    path=cf,                                  
      189 +                    fix_hint=(                                
      190 +                        f"Add '{label}(...)' to {rel}."       
      191 +                    ),                                        
      192 +                ))                                            
      193 +    return results

● The mutual exclusion test for RRFS fails because both rules run. The issue:
  check_makefile_targets runs first (alphabetically check_m... before check_c...
   — wait, actually check_cmake_build comes before check_makefile_targets
  alphabetically. So R2OBLD002 runs first. But the RRFS repo has no
  CMakeLists.txt, so R2OBLD002 returns WARN (no cmake found), not PASS. So PASS
  for R2OBLD002 never fires, meaning R2OBLD001 isn't excluded.

  The issue is that the mutual exclusion only skips if the partner rule passed.
  In RRFS: R2OBLD002 runs first, returns WARN (no cmake), then R2OBLD001 runs
  and finds the Makefile. Both run. We need the exclusion logic to be: if EITHER
   rule ran and produced non-empty results (regardless of status), skip the
  other. Actually, the cleaner approach: skip if the exclusive partner produced
  ANY results at all. Let me rethink this.

  The actual intent: if a repo has Makefiles, skip CMake checks. If a repo has
  CMake, skip Makefile checks. The simplest way: check within the rules
  themselves whether the other build system exists:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Better approach: change mutual exclusion to check if the partner produced any
  results (not just PASS). Let me update the engine:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Still failing. The issue is that check_cmake_build runs first (alphabetically
  check_c < check_m), produces WARN for "No CMakeLists.txt found" — that's not a
   PASS. Then check_makefile_targets runs and produces PASS. Then ran_rule_ids
  has R2OBLD001. But R2OBLD002 already ran. The exclusion needs to prevent the
  second rule from running when the first one found actual build files and
  passed.

  The real fix: both rules should return empty results (not WARN) when their
  build system doesn't exist, letting the other one take over. Then if neither
  exists, only one WARN fires. Let me change both rules to return empty when
  their build system isn't present, and only WARN if the OTHER also doesn't
  exist (but that's circular).

  Simpler approach: make the exclusion check happen inside the rule itself:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  Remaining ambiguities

  1. Optional deep ecFlow mode: The user's prompt mentioned an optional
  deep-parse mode using the ecflow Python library to validate .def files and
  dependency graphs. I did not implement this in Phase 4 because: (a) the ecflow
   library is not pip-installable in most environments, (b) .def file validation
   requires understanding suite topology which is a significant feature, and (c)
   the text-based R2OECF001-005 rules cover the most common compliance issues.
  Should this be deferred to a later phase, or is it important enough to add
  gated behind try: import ecflow?
  2. R2OECF002 head.h trust: Currently R2OECF002 trusts that if %include 
  <head.h> and %include <tail.h> are present, init/complete are handled. This is
   correct for NCO standard head/tail files, but if a project has custom head.h
  without the init call, it would pass falsely. Is this acceptable trust, or
  should we optionally resolve %include directives to check the actual content?
  3. R2OECF005 path exclusion list: Currently flags /lfs/, /work/, /scratch/,
  /gpfs/, /lustre/, /contrib/, /home/. Should this be configurable in
  .r2o-check.yml for sites with different filesystem layouts?

✻ Worked for 12m 3s

❯ Phase 4 looks great — 30 rules, 156 tests, 97% coverage, and the              
  architectural work (mutual exclusion, env split, R2ONAM004 retirement) all    
  landed cleanly. The three questions back are all real design calls. Here's    
  how I'd answer each one.                                                      
                                                                                
  ## 1. Optional deep ecFlow mode — defer, but document the deferral            
                                                                                
  Claude Code's judgment here is correct. The ecflow Python library isn't on    
  PyPI as a standard package (it ships with the ecFlow server, typically via    
  spack-stack or a module load on HPC), so a `try: import ecflow` gate would    
  succeed only on a tiny slice of user environments and silently skip           
  everywhere else. That's worse than not having the feature — it creates "it    
  works on my machine" inconsistency between NOAA HPC users and everyone else.  
                                                                                
  More importantly, the text-based R2OECF001–005 rules catch the high-frequency 
   failure modes. `.def` suite validation (trigger expression parsing,          
  dependency graph acyclicity) catches a different class of bug — one that's    
  valuable but not a v0.1 blocker. Defer it to a dedicated Phase 7 or post-v0.1 
   feature when you can do it properly: publish a separate optional extra (`pip 
   install r2o-check[ecflow]`) with clear documentation about the spack-stack   
  module requirement.                                                           
                                                                                
  **Action:** Add a note to `docs/rules.md` under R2OECF001 explaining that     
  deeper `.def` file validation is planned as a future optional feature         
  requiring the ecFlow library. Add a one-line entry to CLAUDE.md's Phase 6     
  section: *"Consider deep ecFlow mode as a post-v0.1 optional feature,         
  published as `r2o-check[ecflow]` extra. Not in scope for v0.1."*              
                                                                                
  ## 2. R2OECF002 head.h trust — keep the trust, document it, add an optional   
  verification                                                                  
                                                                                
  This is the subtlest of the three. The pragmatic answer is that NCO ships a   
  standard `head.h`/`tail.h` that projects are expected to include unmodified,  
  and R2OECF001 already verifies those includes are present. Trusting them for  
  the init/complete check is consistent with how NCO actually reviews           
  deliveries — they assume standard head/tail are unmodified.                   
                                                                                
  But Claude Code is right that a project with a custom `head.h` could slip     
  through. The right fix isn't to resolve `%include` directives by default      
  (that's a complexity jump), but to add an *optional* check that does the      
  resolution when the include files are present in the repo.                    
                                                                                
  Add **R2OECF006 (WARN)**: If `include/head.h` or `include/tail.h` exists      
  within the repo, parse them and verify they contain `ecflow_client --init`    
  and `ecflow_client --complete` respectively. If the include files don't exist 
   in the repo, skip the rule (they're external/standard and presumed correct). 
   This is a natural refinement rather than a trust change, and it means the    
  behavior is explicit:                                                         
                                                                                
  - `%include <head.h>` present, no `include/head.h` in repo → trust it         
  (current behavior)                                                            
  - `%include <head.h>` present, `include/head.h` exists in repo → verify       
  content                                                                       
                                                                                
  **Action:** Add R2OECF006 to Phase 5's scope in CLAUDE.md. Keep R2OECF002's   
  current trust model and document it explicitly in `docs/rules.md`:            
  *"R2OECF002 trusts that standard NCO head.h/tail.h contain the paired         
  init/complete calls. R2OECF006 verifies this when head.h/tail.h are present   
  in the repo."*                                                                
                                                                                
  ## 3. R2OECF005 path exclusion list — yes, make it configurable               
                                                                                
  This one's a clear yes. Different NOAA HPCs have different filesystem layouts 
   (WCOSS2 uses `/lfs/h2/`, Hercules uses `/work/noaa/`, Ursa has its own), and 
   third-party collaborators at VIMS, URI, Water Institute, and Deltares have   
  entirely different paths. A hardcoded list will either be too permissive      
  (missing paths someone needs) or too strict (false-positiving on legitimate   
  runtime-variable usage).                                                      
                                                                                
  Extend `.r2o-check.yml` with a new field:                                     
                                                                                
  ```yaml                                                                       
  ecflow:                                                                       
    hardcoded_path_prefixes:                                                    
      - /lfs/                                                                   
      - /work/                                                                  
      - /scratch/                                                               
      - /gpfs/                                                                  
      - /lustre/                                                                
      - /contrib/                                                               
      - /home/                                                                  
  ```                                                                           
                                                                                
  The listed values become the defaults. Users can override (replace the list   
  entirely) or extend (add site-specific prefixes). This is also a good forcing 
   function for introducing a configuration namespace pattern — structured      
  sub-keys like `ecflow:` rather than flat top-level keys. Future rule          
  categories will want their own config namespaces (build, env, etc.) and       
  setting the convention now is cheap.                                          
                                                                                
  **Action:** Add to Phase 5 in CLAUDE.md: *"Add namespaced config sections to  
  `.r2o-check.yml`. First use case: `ecflow.hardcoded_path_prefixes` with       
  current hardcoded list as default. Config schema validator must accept both   
  the legacy flat format and the new namespaced format for at least one version 
   for backward compatibility — though since we're pre-v0.1 and no one has      
  pinned a version, breaking it cleanly is fine too; document in CHANGELOG      
  under 'Breaking changes (pre-release).'"*                                     
                                                                                
  Given you're pre-v0.1, break it cleanly. No back-compat.                      
                                                                                
  ## Phase 5 kickoff prompt                                                     
                                                                                
  Phase 5 is the transition from "rule engine" to "shippable product." It's     
  less architecturally heavy than Phase 4 but touches more surface area —       
  formatters, GitHub Action wrapper, R2OECF006, namespaced config. After        
  updating CLAUDE.md with the three decisions above, `/clear` and paste:        
                                                                                
  > Read CLAUDE.md fully. Note three updates from Phase 4 feedback: (1) deep    
  ecFlow `.def` mode deferred to post-v0.1, (2) R2OECF006 added as optional     
  verification when head.h/tail.h exist in repo, (3) `.r2o-check.yml` gains     
  namespaced config sections starting with `ecflow.hardcoded_path_prefixes`.    
  >                                                                             
  > Execute Phase 5:                                                            
  >                                                                             
  > **Refinements first (one commit, "Phase 4 refinements"):**                  
  >                                                                             
  > 1. Add **R2OECF006 (WARN)**: if `include/head.h` or `include/tail.h` exists 
   in repo, parse them to verify they contain `ecflow_client --init` and        
  `--complete` respectively. Skip cleanly when files don't exist. Add fixtures  
  `tests/fixtures/ecflow_custom_head_ok/` and                                   
  `tests/fixtures/ecflow_custom_head_broken/`.                                  
  > 2. Refactor `.r2o-check.yml` to support **namespaced config sections**.     
  Implement `ecflow.hardcoded_path_prefixes` as the first namespaced field with 
   current hardcoded list as default. Update `config.py` schema validation.     
  Wire R2OECF005 to read from the config instead of its hardcoded list. Test    
  with a fixture that overrides the list. Document breaking change in CHANGELOG 
   under "Breaking changes (pre-release)." Do NOT maintain back-compat with the 
   flat format.                                                                 
  >                                                                             
  > **Phase 5 main work — formatters and GitHub Action:**                       
  >                                                                             
  > 3. **JSON formatter** (`src/r2o_check/formatters/json_report.py`):          
  structured output with schema `{version, repo_type, summary: {pass, warn,     
  fail, skip, error}, results: [{rule_id, status, path, message, fix_hint,      
  nco_section}]}`. Add `r2o-check lint <path> --format json` CLI flag. Test     
  schema stability.                                                             
  > 4. **Markdown formatter** (`src/r2o_check/formatters/markdown.py`):         
  PR-comment-friendly output with collapsible sections per category, emoji      
  status indicators (✅/⚠️/❌), summary table at top, and a footer linking to   
  `docs/rules.md`. Add `--format markdown` flag.                                
  > 5. **HTML formatter** (`src/r2o_check/formatters/html.py`): single-file     
  self-contained HTML report with inline CSS, sortable table of results, and    
  category breakdown. Add `--format html` flag. Must work when opened directly  
  in a browser with no server.                                                  
  > 6. **GitHub Action** (`action/action.yml` + `action/entrypoint.sh`):        
  composite action with inputs `path` (default `.`), `standards-version`        
  (default `11.0`), `fail-on` (default `fail`, also accepts `warn` for strict   
  mode), `config-file` (default `.r2o-check.yml`). The entrypoint installs      
  r2o-check from the current checkout (for dogfooding) or from PyPI (once       
  published), runs lint with JSON + Markdown output, and posts the Markdown as  
  a PR comment using `gh pr comment` if running in a PR context. Test the       
  action end-to-end via `.github/workflows/self-check.yml`.                     
  > 7. **Self-check job** in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: add a step that runs   
  `r2o-check lint .` on the r2o-check repo itself. Must pass (repo_type: tool   
  has no applicable rules). Also add a second self-check job that runs the      
  GitHub Action wrapper against the same repo to verify the action composes     
  correctly.                                                                    
  > 8. Update `docs/rules.md` for R2OECF006 and the namespaced config format.   
  Add a new `docs/configuration.md` documenting every namespaced config         
  section. Update `CHANGELOG.md`. Maintain >85% coverage. Commit in logical     
  chunks.                                                                       
  >                                                                             
  > Stop after Phase 5. Report test count, coverage, whether the GitHub Action  
  self-check worked, and any ambiguities remaining before Phase 6 (auto-fix +   
  PyPI release).                                                                
                                                                                
  Phase 5 should be faster than Phase 4 — formatters are mostly templating      
  work, and the GitHub Action is plumbing you've probably written variants of   
  before. The refinement step (R2OECF006 + namespaced config) is the only       
  architecturally interesting part. Expect it to come back in a couple days.

      360 +@register_rule(applies_to=MODEL_REPO_TYPES)                   
      361 +def check_ecf_custom_head_tail(                               
      362 +    repo_path: Path, config: Config                           
      363 +) -> list[LintResult]:                                        
      364 +    """R2OECF006 — Verify custom head.h/tail.h content.       
      365 +                                                              
      366 +    If include/head.h or include/tail.h exists in the repo,   
      367 +    parse them to verify they contain ecflow_client --init    
      368 +    and --complete. Skip when files don't exist (standard     
      369 +    NCO head/tail presumed correct).                          
      370 +    """                                                       
      371 +    results: list[LintResult] = []                            
      372 +    include_dir = repo_path / "include"                       
      373 +    if not include_dir.is_dir():                              
      374 +        return []                                             
      375 +                                                              
      376 +    head = include_dir / "head.h"                             
      377 +    tail = include_dir / "tail.h"                             
      378 +                                                              
      379 +    if head.is_file():                                        
      380 +        content = head.read_text(                             
      381 +            encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"                
      382 +        )                                                     
      383 +        if "ecflow_client --init" in content:                 
      384 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      385 +                status=Status.PASS,                           
      386 +                rule_id="R2OECF006",                          
      387 +                message=(                                     
      388 +                    "include/head.h contains"                 
      389 +                    " ecflow_client --init."                  
      390 +                    " [NCO v11.0 II]"                         
      391 +                ),                                            
      392 +                path=head,                                    
      393 +            ))                                                
      394 +        else:                                                 
      395 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      396 +                status=Status.WARN,                           
      397 +                rule_id="R2OECF006",                          
      398 +                message=(                                     
      399 +                    "Custom include/head.h missing"           
      400 +                    " ecflow_client --init."                  
      401 +                    " [NCO v11.0 II]"                         
      402 +                ),                                            
      403 +                path=head,                                    
      404 +                fix_hint=(                                    
      405 +                    "Add ecflow_client --init to head.h"      
      406 +                    " or use standard NCO head.h."            
      407 +                ),                                            
      408 +            ))                                                
      409 +                                                              
      410 +    if tail.is_file():                                        
      411 +        content = tail.read_text(                             
      412 +            encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"                
      413 +        )                                                     
      414 +        if "ecflow_client --complete" in content:             
      415 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      416 +                status=Status.PASS,                           
      417 +                rule_id="R2OECF006",                          
      418 +                message=(                                     
      419 +                    "include/tail.h contains"                 
      420 +                    " ecflow_client --complete."              
      421 +                    " [NCO v11.0 II]"                         
      422 +                ),                                            
      423 +                path=tail,                                    
      424 +            ))                                                
      425 +        else:                                                 
      426 +            results.append(LintResult(                        
      427 +                status=Status.WARN,                           
      428 +                rule_id="R2OECF006",                          
      429 +                message=(                                     
      430 +                    "Custom include/tail.h missing"           
      431 +                    " ecflow_client --complete."              
      432 +                    " [NCO v11.0 II]"                         
      433 +                ),                                            
      434 +                path=tail,                                    
      435 +                fix_hint=(                                    
      436 +                    "Add ecflow_client --complete to"         
      437 +                    " tail.h or use standard NCO tail.h."     
      438 +                ),                                            
      439 +            ))                                                
      440 +                                                              
      441 +    return results
